## Document 006: 10月贸易数据点评：贸易顺差收窄，AI、价格拉动仍强

### Page 1

[Table_RightTitle] 
宏观研究|宏观跟踪报告

[Table_Title] 
贸易顺差收窄，AI、价格拉动仍强

[Table_ReportType] 
——10月贸易数据点评

[Table_ReportDate] 2026年11月10日

[Table_Summary] ⚫ 事件：10月，按美元计价，我国出口当月同比35.2%，前值
36.3%；进口同比41.6%，前值31%；贸易顺差1053亿美元。 
⚫ 出口增速边际回落，假期错位效应带来一定影响。美国10月
制造业PMI持续走高，9月制造业存货量、新增订单同比均上
行，日本、欧元区、全球制造业PMI小幅回落，海外整体制造
业景气度依然较高。韩国出口增速由83.5%继续攀升至
93.7%，半导体出口保持旺盛。我国高新技术产品、机电产品
出口增速持续上行，产业拉动特征延续。地缘政治扰动和集运
公司控仓的供给侧博弈影响下，集运运价指数延续高位震荡，
航运成本刚性持续。 
⚫ 对美出口持续修复，关税政策短期边际缓和。一方面是低基数
效应影响，另一方面近期贸易政策边际缓和，圣诞节等假日备
货需求均带动了对美出口保持旺盛。对东盟、欧盟、日本出口
累计同比增速持续上行，对俄罗斯、非洲出口高位边际放缓。
我国对韩国出口增速持续走高，AI产业驱动影响依然显著。 
⚫ 成品油、AI产品出口保持旺盛，劳动密集型产品出口持续改
善。集成电路、自动数据处理设备出口金额同比持续攀升，集
成电路出口单价持续走高，价格上涨的驱动作用仍强。下游制
造业出口整体转弱，汽车、船舶、手机等出口增速回落。玩
具、服饰、箱包、家具等劳动密集型产品出口增速保持上行。
上游原材料出口大多上行，成品油出口上行幅度最大，钢材、
铝材增速小幅上行，稀土在政策影响下高位逐步回落。出口管
控政策影响下，肥料出口再度下跌。 
⚫ 进口大幅拉升，贸易顺差缩小，价格效应拉动加强。进口价格
总指数明显高于出口价格指数，贸易顺差收窄显示了输入性通
胀的特征。10月商品价格指数同比上行放缓，原油、半导
体、存储设备等价格上涨为进口的重要支撑。上游能源和AI
相关核心零部件等涨价影响下，国内中下游制造业利润空间或
受到挤压。从行业来看，AI产品进口持续走高，价格影响
下，能源类进口增速上行，农产品进口持续回落。 
⚫ 价格、AI产业拉动仍强，全年贸易增速预期维持高位。10月
出口增速边际放缓，进口受价格驱动显著拉升，增速出现倒
挂。全球AI投资热潮下，半导体、数据处理设备等产品的价
格持续走高，但未来涨价动能或逐步衰减，四季度预期为AI
出口增速的高点。出口为经济的重要拉动项，预期经济达成目
标增速无忧。内需方面需要关注国内政策落地情况。 
⚫ 风险因素：中美贸易关系变动超预期，地缘政治风险发展超预
期，AI产业链订单回落幅度超预期。

事件 
2026年10月， 按美元计价， 我国出口4123.3亿美元， 当月同比35.2%（市场预期33.25%），
前值36.3%；进口金额3070.53亿美元，当月同比41.6%（市场预期30.36%），前值31%；
贸易顺差1052.77亿美元，前值1335.32亿美元。按人民币计价，10月出口当月同比
28.7%，上月为29.6%，进口同比34.7%，上月为24.6%。

1    出口增速放缓，AI产业链拉动作用延续 
出口增速边际回落，假期错位效应带来一定影响，产业拉动特征持续。按美元计价，
10月我国出口4123.3亿美元，当月同比由9月的36.3%回落至35.2%，仍高于市场预期
的33.25%，增速边际放缓，累计同比由21.3%继续上行至22.7%。美国制造业PMI持续
走高，日本、欧元区、全球制造业PMI小幅回落，但仍处临界值上方，海外制造业景
气度依然较高。韩国出口增速由83.5%继续攀升至93.7%，半导体出口保持旺盛，全球
AI投资热潮持续。美国9月制造业存货量、新增订单同比均上行，经济韧性依然较强。
十一长假影响出货节奏错位， 出口增速边际回落。高新技术产品出口累计同比由45.6%
进一步走高至47.8%，AI产业链出口驱动作用仍强。机电产品出口累计同比由29%上行
至30.7%，仍是出口主要推动项。

图表1：韩国出口增速持续走高  （%） 图表2：美国制造业存货持续上行，新增订单反弹(%)

资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所 资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所

万联证券研究所 www.wlzq.cn 第 4 页 共 8 页  
地缘政治扰动和集运公司控仓的供给侧博弈影响下，集运运价指数延续高位震荡。10
月我国出口集装箱运价指数延续高位震荡，指数中枢较9月继续抬升。霍尔木兹海峡
航运持续受阻，欧洲及地中海港口罢工风险升温，以及集运公司持续采取停航控舱策
略以遏制运价下跌，当前航运成本刚性持续。从更新到9月的贸易价格数据来看，我
国出口价格总指数高位小幅回落，矿产品出口价格持续走高，化工相关产品出口价格
持续回落；而进口价格指数持续走高。

图表3：我国对不同国家出口累计同比（%） 图表4：出口集装箱运价指数10月中枢抬升

资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所 资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所

央行上调金融机构外汇存款准备金率，释放稳汇率信号，中期来看人民币仍处升值通
道。美联储10月暂停加息进程，美元指数维持高位震荡。但人民币在10月延续升值趋
势，美元兑人民币在10月中旬已经向下突破6.7。11月3日央行发布公告，将上调金融
机构外汇存款准备金率2个百分点，旨在平抑单边升值预期。如果短期升值过快，可
能会给出口企业造成一定汇兑损失。政策公布后，人民币汇率贬值。短期政策有助于
减缓人民币升值趋势，但从中期来看，人民币走势仍将取决于我国经济数据表现，出
口保持强势，并且年末在企业结汇需求下，人民币汇率仍有支撑。

图表5：人民币升值进程暂缓，美元指数延续高位 图表6：央行外汇占款同比持续上行（%）

证券研究报告|宏观报告

万联证券研究所 www.wlzq.cn 第 5 页 共 8 页  
推动成品油出口持续增长，农产品出口修复上行，但肥料出口再度下跌。从出口商品
来看，前10个月，我国AI产业链出口保持旺盛，集成电路出口金额累计同比由108.1%
继续攀升至111.1%，集成电路出口单价持续走高，自动数据处理设备由54.6%上行至
60.6%，价格上涨的驱动作用仍强。下游制造业出口整体转弱，但仍具韧性，船舶出
口回落2.2个百分点至27.7%，跌幅较大；汽车和汽车底盘由54.3%回落至52.2%，汽车
零件持续上行，新能源汽车出口退税率将于2027年4月下调， “抢出口”的支撑作用持
续。手机、音视频设备出口增速小幅回落。劳动密集型产品出口整体上行，鞋靴、灯
具、陶瓷等跌幅持续收窄，玩具、服饰、箱包、家具等增速保持上行，基数走低和中
美“300亿对300亿”对等降税政策带来一定拉动。上游行业来看，原材料出口大多上
行， 国际油价走高， 成品油出口增速依然是上行幅度最大的分项， 由37.6%上行至43.1%。
钢材、铝材增速小幅上行，稀土在政策影响下高位逐步回落。农产品方面，粮食出口
增速小幅回升， 水产品出口持续上行；肥料出口再度回落，主要受出口管控政策影响。

AI产业链进口持续走高，价格影响下，能源类进口增速上行，农产品进口持续回落。
从进口产品来看，前10个月，我国机电产品进口累计同比增速由33.3%上行至35%，高
技术产品进口累计同比由42.2%上行至44.4%，与出口同步走强。自动数据处理设备增
速上行11.2个百分点至126.2%，上行幅度相对最大，集成电路进口增速也上行1.4个
百分点至58%。国内AI算力基础设施建设和相关硬件加工组装仍处于高速发展期，相
关产品进口需求旺盛。 下游制造业中，汽车零件、 汽车和汽车底盘进口跌幅持续加深，
飞机进口增速低位边际修复。上游资源品进口表现分化，原油、天然气、成品油、煤
及褐煤进口增速上行，价格上涨为主要拉动。铜矿砂、铁矿砂进口增速回落，钢材进
口延续负增，而铜材进口增速高位上行。农产品进口整体回落，其中食用植物油进口
持续上行，大豆、鲜、干水果及坚果增速继续下探。

图表10：煤炭、成品油、天然气进口上行（%） 图表11：AI产业链进口金额增速持续走高（%）

资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所 资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所

3    总结与展望 
价格、AI产业拉动仍强，全年贸易增速预期维持高位。10月出口增速边际放缓，进口
受价格驱动显著拉升，增速出现倒挂。全球AI投资热潮下，半导体、数据处理设备等
产品的价格持续走高， 但未来涨价动能或逐步衰减， 四季度预期为AI出口增速的高点。
出口为经济的重要拉动项，预期经济达成目标增速无忧。内需方面需要关注国内政策
落地情况。

4    风险提示 
中美贸易关系变动超预期， 地缘政治风险发展超预期，AI产业链订单回落幅度超预期。

## Document 009: 10月通胀点评：关注国际能源价格走势的不确定性

10 月通胀点评

关注国际能源价格走势的不确定性   
10 月通胀走势和结构特点与 9 月基本一致；CPI 方面， 同比增速维持在1.0%
下方波动，主要受内需偏弱、服务价格季节性波动、能源和黄金价格输入性
因素影响；PPI 同比增速较 9 月继续上行，能源、化工、有色、计算机通信
等行业仍是主要支撑因素；10 月能源类价格是拉动通胀的重要分项，但考虑
到美国中期选举后，民主党掌握国会可能对中东局势产生重要影响，因此需
要关注能源价格走势对通胀的潜在影响。 
 10 月 CPI 环比持平，同比增长 0.7%，核心 CPI 同比增长 0.8%，服务价
格同比增长 0.7%，消费品价格同比增长 0.8%。 
 从分项同比看 ，10 月同比增长的是交通通信 （5.5%） 、 医疗保健 （2.4%）、
其他用品和服务（1.8%）、教育文化娱乐（ 1.5%）、衣着（1.1%）和生
活用品及服务（ 0.2%），同比增速下降的是居住（ -0.3%）和食品烟酒
（-1.6%），同比增速较 9 月上升的是交通通信和教育文化娱乐，同比增
速较 9 月下降的是衣着、医疗保健、食品烟酒和其他用品和服务。  
 短期内关注中东局势和国际能源价格。10 月影响 CPI 的因素较多，国内
方面，食品价格波动较大，受假期因素影响服务价格季节性上行，海外
方面，能源和黄金价格的输入性影响明显分化。从同比看， 10 月食品价
格影响 CPI 同比下降约 0.49 个百分点 ， 汽油价格影响 CPI 同比上涨约 0.63
个百分点，扣除能源的工业消费品价格影响 CPI 同比上涨约 0.30 个百分
点 ， 服务价格影响CPI 同比上涨约 0.34 个百分点 ； 从影响较大的分项看，
鲜菜 、 猪肉和鲜果合计影响CPI 同比下降约 0.54 个百分点 ， 鸡蛋影响CPI
同比上涨约 0.05 个百分点，牛肉和羊肉合计影响 CPI 同比上涨约 0.09 个
百分点，数据存储设备、平板电脑、计算机和移动电话机四项合计影响
CPI 同比上涨约 0.26 个百分点，黄金饰品影响 CPI 同比上涨约 0.02 个百
分点。我们认为 CPI 分项中，食品、服务等内需相关的产品价格走势相
对稳定，需要关注的是容易受海外因素影响的分项，包括但不限于能源
价格、黄金价格、通信工具产品价格等等。短期内建议关注美国中期选
举后，中东局势是否会发生变化。我们目前暂时维持对年内 CPI 同比增
速在 1%下方平稳波动的观点。 
 10 月 PPI 环比增长 0.7%， 同比增长5.7%， 其中生产资料同比增长7.4%，
生活资料同比下降 0.1%。PPIRM 环比增长 1.1%，同比增长 7.9%。 
 关注能源价格对 PPI 的扰动。10 月 PPI 同比增长 5.7%，涨幅明显高于万
得一致预期 5.4%，结构特点与 9 月较为一致，从分项价格变动看，影响
较大的主要是生产资料中采掘业和原材料工业价格，从行业看主要还是
输入性影响为主的能源化工、受 AI 需求带动的电气设备和计算机通信，
以及季节性需求上升的煤炭 、 燃气等 。 我们认为年底之前PPI 或维持较高
的同比增速，但来自能源价格影响的不确定性上升，建议密切关注美国
中期选举后中东局势可能出现的变化。 
 风险提示：全球通胀二次上行的风险 ；欧美经济回落速度 偏快；国际局
势复杂化。

### Page 2

2026 年 11 月 9 日 10 月通胀点评 2

CPI 
CPI 环比增速连续两个月回落。10 月 CPI 环比持平 ， 增速较9 月下降 0.2 个百分点 。 从分项环比看，
10 月环比上行的包括交通通信（1.0%）、教育文化娱乐（0.3%）、医疗保健（0.1%），环比增速下
降的是生活用品及服务（-0.2%）、食品烟酒（-0.5%）、其他用品和服务（-1.1%），衣着和居住环
比增速持平；10 月分项中较 9 月环比增速上升的仅有教育文化娱乐，环比增速较 9 月下降的是生活
用品及服务、医疗保健、其他用品和服务、衣着、食品烟酒。 
9 月 CPI 同比增速低于万得一致预期。10 月 CPI 同比增长 0.7%，较 9 月下降 0.2 个百分点，核心
CPI 同比增长 0.8%，较 9 月下降 0.2 个百分点 ， 服务价格同比增长0.7%，较 9 月下降 0.1 个百分点，
消费品价格同比增长 0.8%，较 9 月下降 0.2 个百分点。从分项同比看，10 月同比增长的是交通通信
（5.5%）、医疗保健（2.4%）、其他用品和服务（1.8%）、教育文化娱乐（1.5%）、衣着（1.1%）
和生活用品及服务（0.2%） ，同比增速下降的是居住（-0.3%）和食品烟酒（-1.6%），同比增速较 9
月上升的是交通通信和教育文化娱乐，同比增速较 9 月下降的是衣着、医疗保健、食品烟酒和其他
用品和服务。 
10 月食品价格分化加大。从环比看 ，10 月由上月上涨 0.4%转为下降 1.0%， 影响CPI 环比下降约 0.17
个百分点；食品中，气候条件适宜，鸡蛋、鲜菜和鲜果价格分别环比下降 6.3%、5.8%和 0.6%，合
计影响 CPI 环比下降约 0.13 个百分点 ； 市场供应充足 ， 水产品和猪肉价格分别环比下降1.3%和1.0%，
合计影响 CPI 环比下降约 0.04 个百分点；牛肉和羊肉价格分别环比上涨 1.6%和 0.8%，涨幅均有回
落。从同比看，食品价格同比下降 2.9%，降幅比上月扩大 1.2 个百分点，影响 CPI 同比下降约 0.49
个百分点；食品中，鲜菜、猪肉和鲜果价格分别同比下降 16.9%、8.6%和 3.8%，合计影响 CPI 同比
下降约 0.54 个百分点；粮食、食用油和奶类价格同比降幅在 0.5%-1.2%之间，合计影响 CPI 同比下
降约 0.03 个百分点；鸡蛋价格同比上涨 12.3%，涨幅比上月回落 5.1 个百分点，影响 CPI 同比上涨
约 0.05 个百分点；牛肉和羊肉价格分别同比上涨 8.7%和 5.8%，合计影响 CPI 同比上涨约 0.09 个百
分点。 
非食品价格分化，其中既有输入性影响也有季节性影响 。从环比看，10 月服务价格由上月环比下降
0.2%转为环比上涨 0.1%，影响 CPI 环比上涨约 0.04 个百分点；服务中，国庆假期出行人数增加，
宾馆住宿、飞机票和旅行社收费分别环比上涨 6.5%、3.3%和 2.4%，合计影响 CPI 环比上涨约 0.04
个百分点； 工业消费品价格环比上涨0.3%， 涨幅比上月回落0.4 个百分点， 影响CPI 环比上涨约 0.10
个百分点；工业消费品中，受国际市场价格变动影响，国内汽油价格环比上涨 4.2%，涨幅比上月回
落 0.7 个百分点，影响 CPI 环比上涨约 0.14 个百分点；黄金饰品价格环比下降 5.9%，降幅比上月扩
大 3.8 个百分点，影响 CPI 环比下降约 0.03 个百分点。从同比看，10 月汽油价格同比上涨 22.4%，
涨幅比上月扩大 5.8 个百分点，影响 CPI 同比上涨约 0.63 个百分点，带动能源价格同比涨幅由上月
的 7.3%扩大至本月的 9.9%；扣除能源的工业消费品价格同比上涨 1.2%，涨幅比上月回落 0.4 个百
分点，影响 CPI 同比上涨约 0.30 个百分点，其中，数据存储设备、平板电脑、计算机和移动电话机
价格分别同比上涨 45.4%、29.3%、23.0%和 16.7%，四项合计影响 CPI 同比上涨约 0.26 个百分点；
黄金饰品价格同比上涨 4.5%， 涨幅比上月回落18.0 个百分点， 影响CPI 同比上涨约 0.02 个百分点；
服务价格同比上涨 0.7%，涨幅比上月回落 0.1 个百分点，影响 CPI 同比上涨约 0.34 个百分点。 
短期内关注中东局势和国际能源价格。10 月影响 CPI 的因素较多，国内方面，食品价格波动较大，
受假期因素影响服务价格季节性上行，海外方面，能源和黄金价格的输入性影响明显分化。 从同比
看，10 月食品价格影响 CPI 同比下降约 0.49 个百分点，汽油价格影响 CPI 同比上涨约 0.63 个百分
点， 扣除能源的工业消费品价格影响CPI 同比上涨约 0.30 个百分点， 服务价格影响CPI 同比上涨约
0.34 个百分点；从影响较大的分项看，鲜菜、猪肉和鲜果合计影响 CPI 同比下降约 0.54 个百分点，
鸡蛋影响 CPI 同比上涨约 0.05 个百分点， 牛肉和羊肉合计影响CPI 同比上涨约 0.09 个百分点， 数据
存储设备、平板电脑、计算机和移动电话机四项合计影响 CPI 同比上涨约 0.26 个百分点，黄金饰品
影响 CPI 同比上涨约 0.02 个百分点。我们认为 CPI 分项中，食品、服务等内需相关的产品价格走势
相对稳定，需要关注的是容易受海外因素影响的分项，包括但不限于能源价格、黄金价格、通信工
具产品价格等等。短期内建议关注美国中期选举后，中东局势是否会发生变化。我们目前暂时维持
对年内 CPI 同比增速在 1%下方平稳波动的观点。

### Page 4

2026 年 11 月 9 日 10 月通胀点评 4

10 月 PPIRM 环比增长 1.1%，同比增长 7.9%，较 9 月上升 1.0 个百分点；从构成来看，10 月同比增
速上升较多的是燃料、动力类（ 18.6%），化工原料类（ 16.8%）和有色金属材料类（ 16.7%），同
比增速下降的是建筑材料类（-1.9%），黑色金属材料类（-1.1%）和木材及纸浆类（-0.5%）；所有
品类中，同比增速较 9 月上升较多的是燃料、动力类和化工原料类，同比增速下降的是有色金属材
料类，黑色金属材料类、木材及纸浆类和其他工业原料 。 
10 月主要行业中出厂价格同比增速较高的是，煤炭开采和洗选业（ 36.6%），石油和天然气开采业
（35.0%） 和石油 、 煤炭及其他燃料加工业 （29.9%） ， 同比增速较低的是黑色金属矿采选业 （-6.4%），
酒、饮料和精制茶制造业（-5.2%）和电力、热力的生产和供应业（-3.8%）；同比增速较 9 月上升
较多的是石油和天然气开采业，石油、煤炭及其他燃料加工 业和化学原料及化学制品制造业，下降
的仅有有色金属矿采选业，黑色金属矿采选业、有色金属冶炼及压延加工业和造纸及纸制品业 。 
输入性因素和季节性因素影响较大 。10 月 PPI 环比运行的主要特点：一是输入性和季节性因素影响
主要能源相关行业价格上涨。石油和天然气开采业、煤炭开采和洗选业价格、精炼石油产品制造、
有机化学原料制造 、 煤炭加工 、 燃气生产和供应业6 个行业合计影响 PPI 环比上涨约 0.61 个百分点，
超过总涨幅的八成。二是产能治理有序推进、人工智能快速发展带动部分原材料及装备制造行业价
格上行。三是消费品市场供给充足、需求 平稳，价格总体稳定。从同比看，价格上涨的行业中，煤
炭开采和洗选业 、 石油和天然气开采业 、 石油煤炭及其他燃料加工业 、 化学原料和化学制品制造业、
燃气生产和供应业、计算机通信和其他电子设备制造业涨幅比上月均扩大，有色金属矿采选业、有
色金属冶炼和压延加工业涨幅比上月均回落，电气机械和器材制造业涨幅与上月相同，上述 9 个行
业合计影响 PPI 同比上涨约 6.14 个百分点，上拉影响比上月增加 0.64 个百分点。价格下拉影响最大
的 5 个行业为：酒饮料和精制茶制造业、医药制造业、非金属矿物制品业、汽车制造业、电力热力
生产和供应业，降幅比上月均有收窄，合计影响 PPI 同比下降约 0.60 个百分点，下拉影响比上月减
少 0.06 个百分点。 
关注能源价格对 PPI 的扰动。10 月 PPI 同比增长 5.7%，涨幅明显高于万得一致预期 5.4%，结构特
点与 9 月较为一致，从分项价格变动看，影响较大的主要是生产资料中采掘业和原材料工业价格，
从行业看主要还是输入性影响为主的能源化工 、受 AI 需求带动的电气设备和计算机通信 ，以及季节
性需求上升的煤炭、燃气等。我们认为年底之前 PPI 或维持较高的同比增速，但来自能源价格影响
的不确定性上升，建议密切关注美国中期选举后中东局势可能出现的变化。

2026 年 11 月 9 日 10 月通胀点评 5

评级体系说明 
以报告发布日后公司股价/行业指数涨跌幅相对同期相关市场指数的涨跌幅的表现为基准： 
公司投资评级： 
买    入：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内超越基准指数 20%以上； 
增    持：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内超越基准指数 10%-20%； 
中    性：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内相对基准指数变动幅度在-10%-10%之间； 
减    持：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内相对基准指数跌幅在 10%以上； 
未有评级：因无法获取必要的资料或者其他原因，未能给出明确的投资评级。

行业投资评级： 
强于大市：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现强于基准指数； 
中    性：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现基本与基准指数持平； 
弱于大市：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现弱于基准指数； 
未有评级：因无法获取必要的资料或者其他原因，未能给出明确的投资评级。

或恒生中国企业指数；美股市场基准指数为纳斯达克综合指数或标普 500 指数。

國際經濟金融週報內容摘要 
美 
國 
PMI 大幅走升、新屋銷售回穩，消費信心薄弱，美中關係暫現緩和訊號 
經濟 
⚫ S&P Global 公布，9 月美國採購經理人綜合指數(PMI Composite Output 
Index)初值由上月的 56.0 升至 58.4，創逾 5 年新高；服務業及製造業
PMI 分別升至 58.7 及 57.0。在內需強勁帶動下，新訂單與就業同步成
長，惟供應鏈瓶頸及能源成本攀升推升通膨壓力。 
⚫ 8 月美國新屋銷售年化率為 68.4 萬戶，月增 6.4%、年減 2.0%，顯示銷
售較前月回升但需求尚未完全恢復。中西部與南部為主要支撐，惟房價
年減、庫存仍維持 48 萬戶以上，供給相對充裕，後續銷售能否延續仍
待觀察。 
⚫ 9 月美國消費者信心指數降至 48.1，月減 7.0%、年減 12.7%，創近四個
月低點。當前狀況與預期指數同步下滑，反映高物價、燃料價格與貿易
不確定性持續壓抑信心；同時通膨預期升溫，後續消費動能仍待觀察。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 中國國家主席習近平於 9 月 23 日至 25 日訪美並與川普舉行峰會 ， 雙方
同意延長貿易休兵至 2027 年 1 月 10 日，推動部分商品降稅、建立 AI
對話機制及持續磋商稀土供應議題，並延續高層溝通。台灣議題未列入
成果清單，關稅、科技競爭及供應鏈等核心分歧仍未化解，後續談判發
展仍待觀察。 
短評 
⚫ 美國 9 月 PMI 強勁擴張，顯示企業活動與就業動能仍具韌性，但投入
成本與售價上升也反映通膨壓力升溫。相較之下，房市需求尚未全面恢
復，消費信心則明顯轉弱，顯示內需前景仍有隱憂；美中延長貿易休兵
雖有助穩定市場，但核心分歧未解，後續不確定性仍高。 
歐 
元 
區 
9 月景氣增強，進一步推高升息預期 
經濟 ⚫ 歐元區 9 月綜合採購經理人指數(PMI)增 1.1 點至 53.1，服務業 PMI 增 1.4 點至 53，製造業 PMI 則持平於 52.7。 
短評 
⚫ 儘管 9 月經濟表現優於預期，但通膨壓力也同步升溫，再加上先前歐洲
央行的鷹派態度，使得 10 月升息一碼的可能性些微增加，亦強化未來
升息預期。

設備、電動車、軍民兩用技術等關鍵領域，本次降稅實質效益有限。

115.9.29 
一、 美國—PMI 大幅走升、新屋銷售回穩，消費信心薄弱，美中關係暫現緩和
訊號 
標普全球 (S&P Global) 公布， 2026 年 9 月美國採購經理人綜合指數 (PMI 
Composite Output Index)初值由上月的 56.0 升至 58.4， 連續第四個月攀升 ， 創2021
年 7 月以來新高；其中服務業採購經理人指數(Services PMI)由 56.5 升至 58.7，創
59 個月高點，製造業採購經理人指數(Manufacturing PMI) 由 53.9 升至 57.0，創
2022 年 5 月以來最高水準，顯示美國企業活動持續強勁擴張。受國內市場需求穩
健帶動，製造業與服務業新訂單同步加速成長，推升整體產出增速升至逾 5 年來
最快水準，企業亦積極擴增人力，就業成長速度創 2022 年 6 月以來新高。另一
方面，積壓訂單增加及供應鏈延遲加劇，反映產能利用率持續攀升；加以近期油
價上漲推升燃料及運輸成本，使企業投入成本增幅升至 2022 年 10 月以來最高，
帶動售價漲幅回升，顯示通膨壓力有所升溫。儘管如此，企業對未來一年營運前
景仍維持正向看法，反映市場對美國經濟韌性及需求持續擴張仍具信心。 
2026 年 8 月美國新屋銷售經季節性調整年化率為 68.4 萬戶 ， 較上月增加6.4%，
但較去年同期下降 2.0%，顯示新屋銷售較前月回升，惟仍低於去年同期水準。地
區別表現分歧，其中中西部及南部分別較上月增加 84.9%及 6.9%，東北部及西部
則分別減少 36.1%及 15.2%。價格方面，8 月新屋銷售中位價為 39.37 萬美元，較
上月小升 0.4%，但較去年同期下降 5.8%。供給方面，8 月底經季節性調整後待售
新屋數量為 48.3 萬戶，與上月持平，較去年同期減少 2.0%；按目前銷售速度估
算，供給月數為 8.5 個月，低於上月的 9.0 個月，與去年同期大致持平。整體而
言，8 月美國新屋銷售較前月回升，主要受中西部與南部銷售增加帶動，惟仍低
於去年同期，顯示需求動能尚未完全恢復。價格方面，中位價較去年同期下滑，
反映市場價格仍有調整壓力；同時待售新屋維持在 48 萬戶以上，供給月數仍達
8.5 個月，顯示整體供給相對充裕，後續仍須觀察銷售回升是否具持續性。 
密西根大學公布，2026 年 9 月美國消費者信心指數終值降至 48.1，低於上月
的 51.7， 月減7.0%、 年減12.7%， 為近四個月低點 ， 並較2026 年 1 月下降約 15%，
顯示消費者信心持續轉弱。分項來看，當前經濟狀況指數由51.9 降至 50.9，月減

### Page 6

本報告係根據研究資料及各項可靠數據作成，兆豐銀行保留報告內容所有權利，未經本行許可，請勿引
用相關內容。

5

1.9%、 年減15.7%； 消費者預期指數則由51.5 降至 46.3， 月減10.1%、 年減10.4%，
反映民眾對未來景氣與個人財務前景的看法明顯惡化。調查指出，消費者對高物
價的擔憂持續升高，當前與未來一年個人財務看法均較上月轉弱約一成；短期企
業展望也因燃料價格偏高與貿易爭端再度升溫而明顯下滑。政治立場方面，各陣
營受訪者普遍認為年初以來經濟前景轉弱，其中共和黨人信心較 1 月下降 20%，
民主黨人下降 13%。 通膨預期方面 ， 未來一年通膨預期由8 月的 4.0%升至 4.6%，
為今年 6 月以來最高，且高於今年 2 月的 3.4%(美伊爆發衝突之前)及 2024 年各
月水準；長期通膨預期亦由 3.3%升至 3.4%，結束連續三個月持平，並高於 2024
年 2.8%至 3.2%的區間。整體而言，9 月美國消費信心再度降溫，顯示高物價、燃
料價格與貿易不確定性持續壓抑民眾信心，後續仍須觀察通膨預期升溫是否進一
步影響消費動能。 
中國國家主席習近平於 2026 年 9 月 23 日至 25 日訪問美國，並於 9 月 24 日
在華府白宮與美國總統川普舉行峰會。會後，美中雙方未發布聯合聲明，而是分
別公布成果清單，顯示雙方在穩定雙邊關係及維持高層溝通方面取得部分共識，
包括同意建構「基於尊重、公平、對等的建設性戰略穩定關係」、推動 300 億美
元非敏感商品對等降稅安排、建立人工智慧 (AI)或「超級智慧」對話與風險溝通
機制，以及持續就稀土與關鍵礦產供應鏈議題進行磋商。此外，美方並表示中國
將於 2027 年及 2028 年分別進口至少 1,000 萬公噸美國煤炭。值得注意的是，台
灣議題並未出現在雙方公布的成果文件中；另一方面，中方則額外強調兩軍危機
溝通機制及合作搜尋失蹤美軍遺骸等事項。整體而言，本次川習會有助延續美中
對話與降低短期緊張情勢，惟雙方在關稅、科技競爭、供應鏈安全及其他戰略議
題上的結構性分歧仍未完全化解，後續談判進展及雙邊互動方向仍有待持續觀察。 
二、歐元區—9 月景氣增強，進一步推高升息預期 
 歐元區 9 月綜合採購經理人指數(PMI)增 1.1 點至 53.1，且為連續四個月上
升。其中服務業PMI 增 1.4 點至 53，創近 10 個月高點，主要反映 AI 與國防投資
活動增加，帶動商業服務需求，抵銷能源價格上漲對消費購買力的負面影響。製
造業 PMI 則持平於 52.7，不過，德國萊茵河水位下降，沿岸較為發達的化工、製
藥業物流成本增加，因而暫時壓抑製造業上行空間。值得注意的是，由於國際原
油價格回升，綜合投入、產出物價指數皆上升至近 4 個月最高。故儘管 9 月經濟
表現優於預期，但通膨壓力也同步升溫，再加上先前歐洲央行的鷹派態度，使得

### Page 7

本報告係根據研究資料及各項可靠數據作成，兆豐銀行保留報告內容所有權利，未經本行許可，請勿引
用相關內容。

6

10 月升息一碼的可能性些微增加，亦強化未來升息預期，一般認為最終政策利率
(目前為 2.5%)將會落在 3%-3.25%。 
三、日本—9 月綜合 PMI 維持擴張，製造業表現仍具韌性 
 日本 9 月綜合 PMI 由 53.5 降至 52.5，製造業與服務業同步回落，經濟成長動
能較前兩個月的高檔有所降溫。製造業 PMI 下跌 0.8 點至 54.1，雖然產出、新訂
單及出口訂單增速放緩，但外部需求仍相當強勁，尤其 AI 相關需求可能持續帶
動出口表現，加上企業開始重建庫存、供應鏈瓶頸尚未完全解除，預料補庫存需
求將在未來數月繼續支撐生產成長。服務業 PMI 下降 0.9 點至 51.6，受到工資、
運輸及能源成本上升影響 ， 需求動能略有降溫 ， 但積壓訂單與出口業務有所改善 。
另，投入成本與售價漲幅均較前期放緩，顯示通膨壓力正逐步緩解，但整體物價
水準仍維持高檔。此外，企業對未來景氣的信心持續改善，未來活動指數升至七
個月高點，反映企業普遍預期經濟擴張趨勢仍可望延續。 
四、中國—美中貿易延長休戰，惟後續協商成效仍待觀察 
1-8 月工業部門營業收入、利潤年增率分別為 6.6%、17.6%，其中營業收入年
增率較上期(1-7 月)略增 0.1 個百分點，但利潤年增率下降 1.9 個百分點。受惠於
金屬、塑化原物料上漲，加上 AI 需求擴張，以及政府推動高科技產業發展等因
素，營收成長貢獻度較高者包含有色金屬加工(營收、利潤年增 31.1%、71.8%)、
計算機、通信和其他電子設備製造業(營收、利潤年增20.1%、109.9%)、化學原料
及製品(營收、利潤年增 9.1%、51%)。此外，電氣機械及器材業、汽車業營收年
增率雖分別為 11.5%、2.9%，但獲利分別年減 5.2%、16%。非金屬礦物製品業營
收、獲利下滑 9.5、46.7%，黑色金屬加工業營收、獲利分別年減 1.1%、62.4%，
反映產業持續進行減產所致。整體而言，部分產業受惠於短期原物料價格波動，
以及受到政策推動的高科技產業表現較佳，惟部分產業因陷入產能調整週期，或
政府減少支持等因素而表現較弱，產業分化明顯。 
展望未來，儘管美中雙方互相降低等值 300 億美元產品關稅，惟考量雙方每
年貿易總額逾 4,000 億美元，且降稅產品並非半導體、先進製程設備、電動車、
軍民兩用技術等關鍵領域，因此實質效益有限。另外，雖然美中雙方同意延長貿
易休戰，但休戰期只延長兩個月，反映美國仍保留談判槓桿，持續給予中國稀土
供應履約的壓力。後續將關注美中能否在休戰期間就開放市場、科技競爭、供應

7

鏈安全等議題取得進一步共識，若談判不如預期，美國仍可能重新祭出新的反制
措施 ， 而中國亦可能運用稀土作為談判籌碼 ， 經貿關係不確定性仍是較高 。 此外 ，
預計 10 月 8 日舉行歐中貿易會談，若談判未能取得突破，歐盟對中國的貿易限
制恐進一步升級，對中國出口形成額外壓力。 
五、印度及東南亞—亞銀上修亞洲及太平洋經濟體 2026 年 GDP 預估至 4.7% 
亞 洲 開 發 銀 行 (ADB) 最 新 經 濟 展 望 (Asian Development Outlook, ADO 
September 2026 )上修 2026 年亞洲及太平洋地區經濟體 2026 年經濟成長率(GDP)
至 4.7%(7 月預估 4.5%)、2027 年為 4.5%。ADB 認為，亞洲開發中經濟體雖面臨
中東衝突延長、俄烏戰爭再升溫、能源價格高檔震盪及強烈聖嬰現象等逆風，但
在政府刺激政策、民間投資及 AI 帶動科技出口支撐下，區域經濟仍展現韌性。
通膨方面，2026 年通膨率下修至 3.9%，惟能源與糧食價格壓力仍高，2027 年通
膨預測上修至 3.3%。 
區域經濟表現方面 ， 南亞成長最強勁 ，2026 年 GDP 成長率預估上修至 6.4%，
主要受印度內需與投資帶動；東亞則受中國內需疲弱影響，成長維持平穩；東協
國家(ASEAN)上調至 4.7%， 其中越南 、 馬來西亞及泰國受惠於科技與製造業投資
而獲上修。中亞及西亞、太平洋地區則面臨能源及食品價格上漲壓力，成長預測
略為下修至 3.7%、3.0%。(詳下表) 
ADB 認為未來最大風險包括地緣政治衝突升級、聖嬰 現象造成糧食供應衝
擊，以及 AI 相關資產可能出現價格重估。整體而言，AI 驅動的科技投資仍是亞
洲經濟體最重要的成長動能來源，而能源、糧食及氣候風險將成為影響各國通膨
與信用風險的重要變數。

五、台灣—AI 浪潮推升接單動能，外銷訂單再創新高 
8 月外銷訂單達 1,029.6 億美元，首度突破千億美元大關，其中受惠於 ASIC
晶片需求帶動，資通產品接單翻倍、電子產品接單亦大增 83.9%，惟光學器材因
面板需求維持低檔，年減 5%。非科技產品方面，基本金屬年增 26.8%，係因銅箔
及銅箔基板受惠 AI 相關供應鏈需求及銅價續漲，加以鍍鋅鋼需求回溫；機械年
增 28.3%，係因半導體業者增加資本支出，帶動半導體設備、自動化設備需求延
續；化學品年增 15.4%，主因藥品接單成長，加上產業用化學品需求持續增加；
塑橡膠製品年增 6.7%， 主係塑膠原料受原料上漲而推升產品售價 ， 加上部分塑膠
製品訂單回溫。展望未來，經濟部統計處預估 2026 年外銷訂單有望突破 1 兆美
元，在 AI 相關需求持續擴張帶動下，科技產品表現可望維持強勁，而傳統產業
中下游產品亦可望受惠於相關供應鏈需求，惟上游原物料價格競爭激烈，後續仍
須關注第四季消費旺季是否有相關備貨需求。

Advanced Energy Solution (6781.TW)
Growth Intact Despite A Longer HVDC Transition
CITI'S TAKE 
We walked away from AES’s 3Q briefing more confident in the BBU growth 
outlook. Resilient low-voltage demand and higher-power product 
upgrades should sustain growth through the HVDC transition, with 
400V /800V commercialization targeted for mid/end-2027E respectively. 
Rising technical complexity should favor AES’s engineering expertise and 
established customer relationships. We trim our TP to NT$1,550 (from 
NT$1,680) on 25x 2027E P/E from 27x reflecting HVDC timing uncertainty. 
Reiterate Buy as rising BBU penetration and content per rack underpin 
structural growth.
3Q26: strong BBU demand drives growth and mix upgrades lift GPM y/y — 3Q 
sales of NT$5.2bn rose 27% y/y and 9% q/q, driven by strong BBU demand and were 
9% above our forecast. GPM expanded 1.3ppt y/y to 36.3% on mix upgrades, though 
it fell 2.8ppt q/q. Op profits rose 34% y/y with OPM up 1.4ppt y/y to 24.6%. Net profit 
of NT$1.1bn was up 34% y/y, 3% above our forecast but 5% below consensus. Higher 
opex and lower non-op income partly offset the sales beat.
Low voltage BBUs retain a substantial growth runway — Mgmt expects steady 4Q 
growth and remains positive on 2027E, with no downward revisions to customer 
demand plans. Continued datacenter buildout should sustain low-voltage BBU 
procurement while HVDC architectures undergo qualifications. Demand for 3kW 
products remains broad across conventional servers and lower power datacenter 
workloads. AES is working with several new CSPs and overseas SIs, with new 
customer contributions expected in 2027E. Rising GPU power, greater BBU 
penetration and higher content per MW should support volumes and mix-led ASP 
growth. Cell prices are rising, but AES indicates stable procurement through long-
standing relationships with suppliers and will seek to pass on cost increases. 
HVDC progress: commercialization likely in 2H27E — High voltage BBU 
commercialization has been delayed to 2H27E, reflecting product development and 
system integration challenges. Currently samples have been delivered and large-
scale testing is underway for 400V products, with mass production targeted for 
mid-2027E and 800V products are scheduled for end-2027E. Mgmt. sees high 
voltage BBU ASP to be 5-10x higher than low voltage products. We believe 
increasing system complexity and qualification requirements favor AES’s 
established engineering capabilities and CSP relationships, though shipment 
timing and margin contribution require monitoring.

Non-BBU development: new applications broaden the growth base — Mgmt
expects non-BBU growth to outpace BBU growth in 2027E as new projects scale.
LEV demand should gradually recover following the inventory correction. Telecom
application has entered mass production in 2026, with mgmt. expecting the
business to double in 2027E. AGV, AMR, forklift and robotics projects involve
leading international customers, with AGV /AMR products expected to enter mass

production in 2H27E. These projects should diversify revenue and extend AES’s
battery pack expertise into additional markets.
Earnings revisions and TP change —We lift our earnings forecasts by 6% for
2026E mainly to reflect stronger sales strength and GPM in 2026E while we keep
our 2027 /2028E earnings estimates largely unchanged. We slightly lower our TP to
NT$1,550 based on 25x 2027E P/E vs. 27x prior reflecting HVDC timing uncertainty.

### Page 5

Advanced Energy Solution (6781.TW)
09 November 2026 Citi Research
5
Advanced Energy Solution
Company description
Established in January 2020 and listed in March 2021, Advanced Energy
Solution Holdings (AES) is a holding company registered in Cayman Island,
supplying battery packs for LEV (light electrical vehicle such as e-Bikes) and
BBU (battery back-up unit for datacenters) applications. Prior to the
establishment and IPO, AES was a BU focusing on non-IT applications within

its parent company, Simplo Technology (6121.TW).
Investment strategy
We rate AES shares as Buy. Being an industry leader in battery pack design
and manufacturing with a proven track record, we expect AES to benefit from
higher BBU content per rack and deep CSP engagement as the industry
transitions from conventional 48V systems to HVDC architecture. BBU now
contributes > 70% of sales and should remain the key earnings driver as AI
rack power continues to rise. We forecast earnings CAGR of 27% in 2025-

2028E, supported by strong BBU demand, mix upgrade toward high-power
BBU products and op leverage.
Valuation
Our target price of NT$1,550 is based on 25x 2027E EPS on par with its
historical average since listing and supported by a 2025-28E earnings CAGR
of 27%. We use 2027E as the year of valuation base as we think 2027E can
better reflect its earnings power after the commercializarion of HVDC
products.
Risks
Key downside risks to our earnings forecasts that could prevent the shares

from reaching our target price include: 1) slower-than-expected end demand
from e-Bikes post the pandemic and volaitlity in cloud capex deployment that
could impact datacenter BBU sales; 2) component supply
constraints/logistics challenges, which could lead to slower-than-expected
shipment and top-line momentum; and 3) peer competition, which could
impact sales growth and margins negatively, in our view.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

our ratings distribution table for our Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside rating system. However, we reiterate that we do
not consider No View to be a recommendation. For all Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside calls, risk exists that the
catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.

Phison Electronics Corp  | Asia Pacific
3Q26 gross margin miss; our
conservative stance on Nand
modules playing out
Looking back to our February downgrade note: In AI boosts NAND demand, but
Asian module maker margins may soon compress, we noted that Phison's gross
margin could decline in 2Q26 or 3Q26, once it ran out of low-cost inventory.  While
Phison's low-cost inventory cushioned the consumer Nand module margin downside,
its overall NAND module margin started to miss in 3Q26, which means the AI

storage part of its business (eSSD, and cSSD for AI PC, together 40% of revenue)
may see margin decline.
Please see Exhibit 1  for our earnings review. The company's 3Q26 gross margin
declined to 59%, below our 63.5% and the street's 60.3%, also missing the
company's previous commentary about a sustainable gross margin in 3Q26 given its
low cost inventory. The company hopes its AiDaptive module and service business
can pick up and offset the margin pressure in the conventional module business in

the coming quarters.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.

## Document 221: [005380] 현대차(005380) 어려운 업황 속 돋보이는 방어력현대차(005380) 어려운 업황 속 돋보이는 방어력어려운 업황 속 돋보이는 방어력 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[005380] 현대차(005380) 어려운 업황 속 돋보이는 방어력현대차(005380) 어려운 업황 속 돋보이는 방어력어려운 업황 속 돋보이는 방어력 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-30
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

매수 (유지)  
26년 3분기, 물량 감소에도 확인된 이익 방어력 
현대차의 3Q26 매출액은 45조 1,100억원(Y oY -3.4%), 영업이익은 2조 6,770억원
(Y oY +5.5%), OPM 5.9%(Y oY +0.5%p)를 기록했다. 국내 파업 및 영업일수 감소 영
향으로 글로벌 도매 판매량은 92.1만대(Y oY -11.3%)까지 감소했으나, HEV 중심의 
고부가 차종 Mix 개선, 기말환율 하락에 따른 판매보증비(Y oY -1.2조원) 감소 효과
로 수익성은 오히려 개선됐다. 특히 HEV 판매 비중은 18.2%(Y oY +2.7%p)까지 상
승했다. 반면 EV 판매는 전년 대비 감소세가 이어지며 지역별 수요 둔화와 경쟁 심
화 영향이 지속됐다. 
연간 눈높이는 낮아졌지만, 믹스 개선은 지속 
현대차는 글로벌 수요 둔화와 생산 차질을 반영해 2026년 글로벌 도매 판매 목표를 
기존 415.8만대에서 400만대로, 영업이익률 가이던스를 기존 6.3~7.3%에서 
5.5~6.5%로 하향 조정했다. 다만 북미를 중심으로 HEV 판매가 견조하게 유지되고 
있으며, 하반기 주요 신차 투입과 전동화 라인업 확대를 통해 믹스 개선 기조는 이
어질 전망이다. 중장기적으로는 로보택시 공급 확대와 Atlas 상용화 등 Physical AI 
사업도 계획대로 진행 중으로, 단기 판매 성장 둔화에도 자동차 본업의 수익성 방어
와 신사업 모멘텀은 유효한 것으로 판단한다. 
투자의견 매수 유지, 목표주가 460,000원 하향 
글로벌 완성차 업체들의 수익성 부담은 확대되고 있다. 3분기 자동차 부문 영업이익
률은 Mercedes-Benz 2.5%, Stellantis 0.7%, Volvo -5.6%, VW -7.8%를 기록했다. 
유럽 내 BEV 판매 확대에도 중국 전기차 업체들의 공격적인 해외 진출과 가격 경
쟁, 중국 시장 부진이 동시에 나타나면서 전통 OEM의 수익성 압박이 심화되고 있
다. 이러한 환경에서 현대차는 HEV 중심의 유연한 파워트레인 전략과 북미 중심의 
Mix 개선을 통해 5% 후반의 수익성을 유지하고 있다는 점이 상대적으로 부각된다.

동 자료에 언급된 종목의 지분율 1%이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
당사는 상기 명시한 사항 외 고지해야 하는 특별한 이해관계가 없습니다.

## Document 229: [005930] 삼성전자(005930) 현저한 저평가삼성전자(005930) 현저한 저평가현저한 저평가 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[005930] 삼성전자(005930) 현저한 저평가삼성전자(005930) 현저한 저평가현저한 저평가 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-06
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

00

삼성전자 (005930/KS) 
현저한 저평가 
3Q26 영업이익 106 조원 예상 
삼성전자의 3Q26 영업이익을 106조원 (+18% QoQ)으로 예상한다. 이는 기존 당사 
전망치 111 조원 대비 4.5% 하향된 것이다. 원/달러 환율 가정을 기존 1,450원에서 
1,400원으로 3.4% 하향, MX/NW 사업부의 적자 폭을 확대 반영 (-0.5조원 -> -
1.4조원)했기 때문이다. 주가의 핵심인 메모리 판가는 DRAM +17%, NAND +15%
로 오히려 예상을 소폭 상회할 것으로 예상된다. 출하는 수요 강세에도 불구하고 재고 
부족, HBM 확대에 따른 제한적 공급 여력으로 DRAM +4%, NAND +7%의 기존 예
상을 유지한다. HBM은 분기 최대 출하를 경신하기 시작할 것이다. 또한 AI 강세로 파
운드리 수주 및 가동률의 동반 상승이 지속 중이다. 성과 충당금 반영 등에 따라 단기 
흑자전환 가시성은 제한적이나, DS 사업부 전체적으로 강화되고 있는 국면이다.

2027 년 568 조원 (+55% YoY) 전망 유지. 환율 하락 반영 
SK증권은 삼성전자의 2027년 영업이익 전망치를 568조원으로 유지한다. 원/달러 환
율 가정을 기존 1,440원에서 1,340원으로 7% 하향했음에도, 실적 전망을 유지하는 
이유는 AI 메모리 수요 강세 속 공급 부족 심화, LTA에 따른 Dual market 효과에 의
한 Commodity 메모리 가격 강세, 2027년 HBM 판가 강세 (+103% YoY) 와 출하 
강세, 수익성 회복의 동반, 파운드리 적자 폭 축소의 본격화 전망 때문이다. 2027년 메
모리 출하는 DRAM +22%, NAND +16%, 판가는 DRAM +38%, NAND +28%
를 예상한다. 시장에서 우려하는 메모리 가격 하락, 감익 시나리오는 없을 것이다.

차별적 실적과 주주환원 본격화. 현저한 저평가 
SK증권은 메모리 업황과 삼성전자의 주가가 현저히 저평가 되어있다고 판단한다. 삼성
전자의 2027E P/E는 4.2X에 불과하며, 이익 창출력은 글로벌 최상위권이다. 주주환
원 정책 또한 기존의 FCF 50% 환원이 향후에도 유지될 것으로 가정한다면, 2027년 
193조원, 2028년 222조원으로 현재 시가총액 (우선주 포함) 대비 각각 11%, 13% 
수준에 육박한다. 성장을 위한 투자를 확대하고 있음에도, 대규모의 환원이 가능하다. 
LTA는 AI 시대 메모리 수요의 질과 실적 가시성을 제고시키며, 이는 주주환원의 지속
가능성을 의미한다. 메모리 가격의 높은 기저, LTA 물량의 계약 가격 적용을 감안하면, 
가격 상승률 자체는 안정화될 것이다. 다만 실적 안정성, 가시성, Duration을 확보하기 
시작했다는 점이 훨씬 중요한 가치이며, 아직 시장의 Playbook에 없을 뿐이다. 시대는 
변하고 있고, 시장도 변해야 한다. 투자의견 BUY, 목표주가 610,000원을 유지한다. 
2026-10-06

반도체. 한동희 / donghee.han@sks.co.kr / 3773-8826

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
ECONOMIC INSIGHT
As expected, the RBA Monetary Policy Board (MPB) lifted
the cash rate 25bps to 4.85% at its November meeting.
This follows the increase at the previous (late-September)
meeting and is a marked turnaround from the outlook back
in August. The post-meeting media statement cited higher

global energy prices resulting from the Middle East conflict,
tighter domestic capacity pressures and an increase in
inflation expectations for the decision.
However, the decision was a split vote, 6 votes to 3, a
possibility we have flagged for some time. In the post-meeting
media conference, Governor Bullock explained that the
dissenting minority put more weight on the downside risks and
did not see signs of wages growth picking up as much as the

staff forecasts implied. Those members were reportedly more
confident that slower growth would prevent higher inflation
expectations becoming entrenched.
Financial conditions are restrictive and this is expected to
bring inflation down over time. Because the RBA assesses the
risks to the inflation outlook to still be skewed to the upside,
the outlook for coming meetings will still be for a decision
between holding and hiking. But we note that the post-meeting

statement omitted September’s language “including increasing
the cash rate target further if needed” from the commitment to
do what is necessary to get inflation down.
In the post-meeting media conference, the Governor
commented that after five hikes, this is probably a good time
to “take stock”. Together with the dissents, we read this as
confirming our current view that the MPB will keep rates
on hold in December. There is also considerable data flow

between now and then, which could go either way, including
the Q3 national accounts.
Similarly, a lot could happen between now and February, but
we see it as the next most likely timing for a hike, should one
eventuate. We still see that as a risk rather than a base case.
Revised forecasts published in the accompanying Statement
on Monetary Policy (SMP) show underlying inflation lingering
around current rates for longer. This contrasts with the
previous SMP forecasts in August, which showed trimmed

mean inflation returning to the 2–3%yr target range by late
2027 and a little below the 2.5%yr target midpoint in 2028.
We note, however, that the conditioning assumptions on oil
prices, which are based on futures prices, were at a local peak
at the time the forecasts were finalised.
RBA hikes cash rate to new high of 4.85%, a December pause likely
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group
The RBA’s GDP growth forecasts for 2027 and 2028 were
both revised down –0.3%pts to 1.3% for 2027 and 1.5% in

2028, despite an upward revision to the near term. This is
well below even the RBA’s downbeat view of potential output
growth. Within the GDP aggregate, recent misses and leading
indicators have required upward revisions to the near-term
outlooks for business and dwelling investment, and downside
revisions to public demand. These revisions were offset further
out with downward revisions to the 2028 outlook for business
and dwelling investment and an upward revision to public

demand. Household consumption forecasts were revised down
across most of the profile.
With the unemployment rate already at 4.7% in August and
September – a rate the RBA did not forecast until end-2027 in
its August SMP forecasts – the RBA has lifted its unemployment
forecast to show a peak of 5.1% in at end-2028, the end of the
forecast horizon. This largely reflects stronger labour supply,
with forecasts for employment growth essentially unchanged.

We have repeatedly flagged that cost-of-living pressures are
likely to keep people in the labour force and thus boost the
participation rate. The RBA had previously not factored this
adequately into its forecasts but acknowledged in the SMP
that this mechanism had kept participation higher than it had
expected at the time of the August SMP.
However, because inflation surprised to the upside recently,
this additional labour supply has had no bearing on the

RBA’s assessment of capacity pressures. The RBA assesses
the domestic economy to still be a little tight, and tighter
than previously judged. The RBA’s framework for assessing
the degree of spare capacity or tightness in the economy
necessarily translates an upside surprise on inflation (or unit
labour costs) into a conclusion that the economy must be
tighter than previously judged. Indeed, the RBA’s estimate
of the NAIRU (unemployment rate consistent with inflation

stable at target) has been revised up to 4.75%.
The RBA highlighted that elevated inflation expectations may
be contributing to higher realised inflation. Consumer inflation
expectations have started to lift again but remain below where
they peaked earlier this year. However, the RBA appears
“RBA lifts cash rate 25bps to 4.85% in split
vote, citing “broadly based” domestic inflation
pressures, Middle East conflict and AI boom."

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
to be putting more attention on the inflation expectations
of firms and the frequency of mentions of pricing in their
liaison program. This is putting a lot of weight on proprietary
information that is not clearly validated by other, longer-
running data sources.

The AI boom is heavily cited in the SMP and other post-
meeting communication. The RBA has lifted its forecasts
for major trading partners, especially high-income Asian
economies that are the centre of high-tech manufacturing.
Demand for AI-related goods is also cited as a driver of both
global and domestic inflation. And although the RBA has
revised up its near-term forecasts for business investment
on the back of strong data centre activity, it is working on

the assumption that Australia does not become ‘a significant
regional hub for data centres’. Nor does it allow for any boost
to productivity trends from the technology, even in 2028. This
is a very different view of the risks from AI than the RBNZ
expressed last week. For the RBA, it is all about inflation, a
little bit about pressure on domestic construction resources,
and nothing about either an ugly unwind of the current
financing boom or a future productivity dividend.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
THE WEEK THAT WAS
Cliff Notes: whiplash
total to $11.1bn. This is another clear marker of the scale of
Australia’s data-centre investment boom, which should feature
prominently in both business investment and imports in the Q3
National Accounts.
Offshore, the world has been focused on the US mid-term

elections. In the House, Senate and a series of State Governor
races, the Democrats experienced resounding victory. Come
2027, the Democrats will have a comfortable majority in both
houses of Congress and are expected to make use of this
strength to respond to voter concerns, particularly over cost-
of-living pressures. The election result also limits President
Trump’s ability to fund an extended military action against
Iran. Notably, both immediately before and after the election,

President Trump and the administration have exhibited a greater
interest in furthering negotiations with Iran. It is therefore not
a surprise that Brent oil has backed off its most recent highs
around USD118 last Friday, although the scale of the drop to
near USD93 yesterday is arguably too optimistic. Brent has since
traded back up to above USD99, still 16% below Friday’s peak.
While the initial reaction to the US election result was a
robust broad-based rally in major equity indexes, concerns

over the potential for tighter regulation of AI and the related
infrastructure buildout jolted US equities lower overnight – the
Nasdaq falling 5.5% and S&P500 4.4%, both a multiple of
the prior session’s gains. That said, US term interest rates and
FOMC rate expectations have also been scaled back; so, while
tech may come under greater scrutiny, a foundation for better
outcomes across the rest of the US economy may become
evident in time. US data out this week continued to suggest

their economy is in robust health overall. And, if tensions in
the Middle East abate, the FOMC’s concerns over US inflation
should subside. Gaining enough comfort for the FOMC to not
hike in December is a high bar, but the probability of further
hikes in 2027 should continue to fall.
Turning then to the UK, the Bank of England raised Bank Rate
by 25bps to 4.00% at their November meeting as expected.
On the Bank's baseline expectation, UK inflation is forecast to

rise from 3.5%yr at September to 5.00% by mid-2027. Activity
data has also surprised to the upside in recent months, though
"there remains limited evidence of knock-on effects on other
prices and wages".
In Australia, the RBA delivered another 25bp rate increase
at its November meeting, taking the cash rate to 4.85%. The
move was widely expected, reflecting the Board’s continued
concern around inflation following the recent run higher in
global energy prices, firmer domestic capacity pressures and

signs that inflation expectations have edged up. Inflation
is now not expected to return to the mid-point of the
target band until mid-2028. This is despite weaker growth
expectations and the unemployment rate trending up to
5.1% by end-2028 on the RBA’s forecasts.
Importantly, though, the decision was not unanimous, with
three members preferring to remain on hold given downside
risks to activity and the restraining influence of slower growth.
Governor Bullock’s suggestion that it was time to “take stock”

after five rate increases also struck a less hawkish note,
while the statement dropped September’s explicit reference
to raising rates further “if needed”. In our view, February
represents the next plausible opportunity for another hike, but
we see this only as a risk.
Policy’s effect on housing is becoming increasingly visible.
Cotality home prices fell a further 1.2% in October to now
be 6.7% below March’s peak. The cumulative price decline
is approaching the scale of previous housing corrections but

appears to have regained momentum after September’s rate
increase. Dwelling approvals  saw a 5.1% headline gain in
September, but this was driven by volatile unit approvals while
detached house approvals fell sharply, suggesting higher rates
and the established-market downturn are beginning to weigh
on the construction pipeline.
Official data on nominal household spending  meanwhile
rose 0.6% in September and 2.1% for Q3; however, much of
the recent acceleration looks to be price-driven, particularly

for fuel. Real spending volumes expanded by a much more
modest (but still solid) 0.7% in Q3.
The Westpac-DataX Consumer Panel provides a granular look at
income, spending and saving flows. The latest update suggests
that, beneath the resilient headline story, households are
increasingly being squeezed, particularly those with low savings
buffers. With another rate increase now delivered, housing
wealth declining and sentiment weak, Q3 increasingly looks like

the high-water mark for growth in consumer spending.
Lastly for Australia, in September the goods trade surplus
widened sharply to $2.8bn, with non-monetary gold trade
yet again adding considerable noise month-to-month. The
most notable development was another exceptionally strong
result for data-processing equipment imports, taking the Q3
Elliot Clarke, Head of International Economics
Ryan Wells, Economist

policy this week, in part due to price pressures
emanating from the Middle East."

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
In the press conference, Governor Bailey recognised the
nascent improvement in energy price pressures over the
past 48 hours and the effectiveness of monetary policy but
also conveyed that "Unless progress towards resolving the
conflict is made, or there is other news which softens the risk

to inflationary pressures, a further increase in Bank Rate may
be warranted". Governor Bailey's tone and comments made in
the minutes by other Committee members did not suggest this
was a decision that needs to be made in December, but rather
a risk to be judged as additional information is received. This
points to further tightening being more likely in early 2027
than before, and only if conditions / risks warrant.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FOCUS ON NEW ZEALAND
No deals are done
This week’s developments underscore our message that
a rate hike at the RBNZ’s December meeting is far from a
done deal. New Zealand’s labour market remains softer
than expected, and that will be noted by those on the

policy committee who question the extent of second-round
inflation pressures. The rout in the US mid-term elections
has also taken significant heat out of oil prices for now,
although it’s unclear whether that situation will hold through
to next month. Our view on the endpoint for this OCR cycle
remains unchanged, but there’s a growing risk that the RBNZ
could slow the pace of tightening in the new year.
The September quarter labour market surveys pointed to

ongoing softness in the jobs market. The unemployment
rate rose from 5.6% to 5.7%, continuing a four-year run
of increases, and reaching its highest level since 2015.
Employment was softer than expected, and wage growth
remains subdued despite increasing cost-of-living pressures.
The Household Labour Force Survey (HLFS) showed a surprise
0.1% fall in employment in the September quarter, after a
downwardly revised 0.3% increase in the June quarter. That

went against the Monthly Employment Indicator (MEI) measure
of filled jobs, which had suggested some upward momentum
emerging in the jobs market (and notably, the HLFS measure
had outpaced the MEI for the previous nine straight quarters).
The fall in HLFS employment was accompanied by a fall in
the labour force participation rate from 70.6% to 70.4%.
These co-movements essentially reversed the surprisingly
large increases that we saw in the June quarter, which we had

suggested at the time were more likely due to sampling error
in the survey rather than a genuine surge.
We generally recommend focusing on the unemployment rate,
which is less vulnerable to sampling errors – and the message
here is one of softness. Our best interpretation is that, to the
extent that jobs are growing, it’s not fast enough to absorb the
growth in the population (which itself is a fairly low bar, as net
migration remains at a low ebb).

Even with the economy returning to growth over the last
year or so, hiring activity has remained weak. This may be
an ongoing legacy of employment trends in previous years.
Businesses have told us that they felt burned by the severe
skill shortages that developed over 2021-23, and looked to
hold on to workers to the extent they could during the 2024-
25 downturn. The flipside of this is that they may now find
themselves overstaffed even as activity picks up again.

This shows up most strikingly in labour force transition rates.
The rise in unemployment over the last few years has not
been driven by an increase in job losses, but by accumulation
of people who, once out of work, are finding it increasingly
difficult to get back in again. That also extends to young
people trying to enter the workforce for the first time – youth
unemployment of around 25% is the highest in the history of
the survey going back to 1986.

The Labour Cost Index (LCI) rose by 0.5% in the September
quarter, in line with our expectations overall but a bit weaker
in the details – private sector wages were up by 0.5%, while
there was a surprisingly large 0.7% increase in public sector
wages. There was a notable jump in the number of employers
citing cost-of-living adjustments as a reason for increasing pay
rates – although the size of those increases varied a great deal,
with many substantially less than the headline inflation rate.

Potential RBNZ implications
The labour market data was unequivocally weaker than
expected by either us or the RBNZ. While likely not a
gamechanger for the cycle overall, it will add ammunition to the
arguments of the doves on the Monetary Policy Committee that
a pause in interest rate rises will make sense soon – perhaps in
December or early next year. It’s likely the case that interest rate
markets were over-pricing the odds of a hike at the 9 December

MPS, so the subsequent pullback in pricing makes sense.
The message from the RBNZ in the October meeting was to
be careful about pricing in "done deals", and that the outlook
Michael Gordon
Senior Economist

### Page 7

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
7
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
will be data-dependent. As we noted at the time, alongside
measures of inflation expectations, the key domestic data
to watch ahead of the RBNZ’s 9 December meeting will
be indicators of what is happening to excess capacity (the
output gap), as this will be a key determinant of the Bank’s

assessment of the outlook for inflation over the coming year.
The RBNZ looks at a broad suite of indicators of labour market
slack, although their research found that only four of them
seem to have predictive power: the unemployment rate,
the vacancy to unemployment ratio, the QSBO measure of
labour as the main limiting factor to growth, and the rate of
transition between jobs. The first three of these all pointed
to a softening in the September quarter. (The fourth one is

calculated from income tax data, so it’s not publicly available
and even the RBNZ receives it only with a lag.)
In the eyes of the dovish MPC members, the softness in
the labour market indicators will likely push in the direction
of having greater comfort with the medium-term inflation
outlook. The outcome of the election, with the possibility of a
return to a dual inflation/employment mandate, could increase
the prominence of these factors. This likely won’t be an explicit

factor in the RBNZ’s reasoning at its December review, but it
may be in the minds of individual MPC members.
That said, it’s important to remember that in the RBNZ’s
forecasting framework, the GDP figures are the main
determinant of excess capacity in the economy. The most
recent GDP data tells us that the output gap probably
narrowed a bit through the June quarter, mainly reflecting
historical revisions. In light of the latest labour market figures,

there may be some downside risk to the outlook for Q3 GDP –
but there are conflicting indicators there, so it’s not that clear.
At last month’s surprise on-hold OCR decision, Governor
Breman lamented that there was no protocol for the RBNZ
to be able to signal how it was reading the economic data –
in particular the softer than expected Q3 CPI print – ahead
of the policy meeting. In that light, it will be interesting to
see whether they use some already-scheduled speaking

engagements to signal what they make of the labour market
data. Top of mind is Chief Economist Conway's 10 November
speech to the UBS Australasia Conference (pointedly titled
“How is the NZ economy tracking”). Governor Breman is also
speaking to the Institute of Directors on 19 November.
Our view remains that the December OCR hike is more likely
than not. But there will be a clear debate to be had between
the MPC hawks and doves, and it may again come down to a

vote. We still expect a 4% peak in the OCR for this cycle, but
the time taken to get there could be more flexible. Takes along
the lines of "3% is the terminal rate" seem rather hasty on the
basis of a single data release.
Aside from how the Committee interprets the labour market
data, the key considerations in December will be: the RBNZ’s
surveys of inflation expectations by households and businesses;
whether the US mid-term elections lead to a sustained de-

escalation in the Middle East and a pullback in oil prices; and
whether developments in the AI space exacerbate some MPC
members’ concerns about the global economic environment.
And then of course there’s the result of the General Election,
with voting closing on Saturday evening. The actual formation
of a government is likely to take weeks of negotiation, so the
outcome may not be known by the 9 December MPS but at least
the range of possibilities should be clearer. We’ll publish our

initial thoughts on the election results early on Monday morning.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
NZ: Oct Retail Card Spending (%mth)
Nov 12, Last: –0.4, Westpac f/c: 1.4
We’re forecasting a 1.4% rise in retail spending in October.
While that would be a solid increase, it’s in large part due to
the 11% rise in petrol prices over the past month. Stripping out

fuel spending, we’re forecasting a 0.6% rise in core spending
categories. Core spending has been very choppy recently,
and the increase we’re assuming follows falls in the past few
months. A result in line with our expectations would leave the
trend in core spending relatively flat since the start of this year
with the rise in fuel prices weighing on spending in other areas.
AUS: Nov Westpac-MI Consumer Sentiment (index)
Nov 10, Last: 84.4
Consumer sentiment fell 4.7% to 80.4 in Oct, returning to the

deeply pessimistic levels seen earlier in the year. Responses
over the course of the survey week showed a very sharp
deterioration after the RBA rate hike.
The Nov survey is in the field over the week ended Nov 7.
Interest rates will again factor with the RBA raising the cash
rate by another 25bps to 4.85%, an 18yr high. Average pump
prices also pushed higher, the national average just under
$2.40/litre, while the housing market downturn has continued

to broaden and deepen. The US mid-term election results may
have some effects on sentiment locally as well.
AUS: Q3 Housing Finance Approvals (%qtr)
Nov 11 Last: –5.2, Westpac f/c: –10
Market f/c: –8.5, Range: –15 to –5
The total value of housing finance approvals fell The value of
new dwelling finance approvals excl. refinance fell 5.2%qtr,
the largest quarterly drop in three years. Annual growth eased
to 7.3%yr. The weakness was accounted for mainly by lower

lending volumes: loan numbers were down 4.0%qtr (–0.6%yr),
while average loan size also contributed negatively.
Q3 is expected to see an even bigger decline with the full
impact of interest rate rises and tax policy changes showing
through. The value of both loan enquiries and turnover fell by
around 10% in the quarter. We expect total finance approvals
to show a 10% drop as well, with investor loan approvals likely
to be down 16%qtr and owner occupier approvals down 6%qtr.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
US: Oct CPI (%mth)
Nov 11, Headline, Last: 0.6, Market f/c: 0.3, Westpac f/c: 0.4
US consumer prices rose by 0.6% in September, although
half of the gain was due to energy inflation, with core prices
instead up 0.3%. Over the year, prices were reported to have

risen 3.7%yr overall and 2.6%yr excluding food and energy.
Constructively, ex food, energy and shelter, annual inflation
met the FOMC’s medium-term target of 2.0%yr.
Come October, energy price pressures are set to recede
given the pre-election run higher in oil prices came late in the
month. A softer print for shelter is also likely to moderate core
momentum. We see a 0.4% gain for the headline measure and
a 0.2% result for core inflation as most probable. Looking to

2027, disinflation is expected to continue at a measured pace
towards the medium-term target.

## Document 285: Blue Wave_ Democrats sweep the US midterms

Source PDF: all report pdf/Blue Wave_ Democrats sweep the US midterms.pdf
Report source: 渣打銀行 (Market Watch)
Date: 2026-11-05
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Blue Wave: Democrats 
sweep the US midterms  
Summary 
Event: The Democrats gained control of the House of 
Representatives and the Senate at the US midterm elections 
that took place this week.  
Our View: With the US government now no longer under 
unified control of the Republicans, gridlock has returned to 
Washington. We believe investors should not overreact to 
these results and should stay invested. 
In equities, investors should  continue to diversify their 
equity exposure into more defensive sectors.  As we 
transition to mid -cycle for the equity markets , we expect 
stocks to continue to deliver positive returns in the 8 -10% 
range per annum, a downshift from the torrid pace of gains in 
the earlier part of the cycle.  
In bonds, income remains attractive given recent spread 
widening. We favour non -hyperscaler investment grade 
corporate bonds at the three to seven -year part of the 
curve. We continue to expect the US dollar to remain range-
bound in the near  term, as we expect two more rate hikes 
from the Fed, but as this mini rate hike cycle ends in the 
second quarter of 2027, the USD index (DXY) is likely to 
depreciate towards 98.5 as markets start to anticipate a Fed 
rate cut in the second half of next year.  
Oil is likely to rebound in the short -term on heightened 
geopolitical risk, but as supply chains adapt to the current 
situation and Congress exercises oversight on defense 
spending, WTI prices are likely to drift towards our 12-month 
target of USD 80 per barrel.

Democrats made solid gains across the Senate, House 
of Representatives and State Governors 
Results reported as at 7:45PM Singapore Time

Source: The Wall Street Journal, Standard Chartered

### Page 2

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 5 November 2026

Major implications of Democrat control of 
Congress   
1) Fiscal expansionary policies may slow. Democrats are 
now in position to block any new tax cuts or spending plans 
from the Trump administration. At the same time, any fiscal 
policies proposed by Democrats may also face Trump’s veto. 
Hence the fiscal trajectory of the US will likely remain stuck  
over the next two years. As we get into the second half of 
2027, the US debt ceiling will once again loom, and gridlock 
could potentially bring back government shutdowns. 
However, as they have always done in the past, compromise 
will ultimately prevail, in our opinion.  
2) Trump remains in control of foreign policy.  With the 
midterms now out of the way, could Trump decide to re-start 
combat operations with Iran? We believe there is a risk that 
US and Israel – now that both countries’ have concluded 
important elections – will once again be tempted to restart 
combat operations to force an end to the conflict. However, 
the duration of any renewed hostilities may be limited by 
Congress next year as the Democrats will likely withhold 
funding for further military operations. In the near -term, oil 
prices may experience renewed volatility before stabilising at 
a lower level next year. The introduction of new tariffs on 
trading partners may prove harder with Democrat oversight. 
3) AI data centre moratoriums could grow, though model 
development regulatory changes would be harder to 
pass. While the headlines have focused on the House and 
Senate results, one important development has also 
occurred at the gubernatorial level. Notably, Democrats have 
won the governor races in Texas and Georgia, two big data 
centre states. As data centre build out permits are largely 
decided at the local state level, this will have important 
ramifications for build out plans next year.  However, any 
Democratic proposals to regulate AI model developments 
would need to be signed off by President Trump, who 
continues to wield veto power on any such proposals.   
Asset class strategy 
Equities: Remain invested and continue to diversify .  
While investors initially overreacted, President Trump’s 
relatively conciliatory response has provided some welcome 
relief, helping markets recover strongly from their pre-market 
lows. Hence, we recommend investors avoid 
overreacting, remain invested and continue to diversify 
their equity exposure across sectors and regions.  
A few themes stand out from the early pre-market reaction to 
the mid-term elections. On the positive side, defensive stocks 
continue to fare well, while themes favoured by Democrats 
are also outperforming. Clean energy is a notable 
beneficiary, with solar s tocks among the biggest winners

Policy uncertainty and a change in the innovation cycle 
is consistent with our view of a mid-cycle transition, 
where equity returns moderate but remain positive

Standard Chartered Bank

today. Affordability-related themes are also rallying, including 
companies that could benefit from potential tariff rollbacks. 
Medicaid-related stocks are also moving higher on 
expectations that potential policy changes could provide a 
tailwind for the healthcare sector. 
On the negative side, semiconductor weakness continues 
amid concerns around potential data centre moratoriums. 
Our AI capex estimates remain conservative relative to 
consensus, but with a potential reset in expectations around 
data centre spending, investo rs should remain selective. 
After downgrading semiconductors last month, we have 
maintained a conservative stance on the industry, with a 
lower recommended allocation compared to September. 
While we believe the mid -term election outcome is not a 
complete surprise to markets, particularly given pre -election 
polling and the recent weakness in both the S&P 500 and 
Nasdaq, the overnight correction highlights how complacent 
investors had become, with the VIX initially surging more than 
10% in pre -market trading, although down from intra -day 
highs after President Trump’s comments. 
As we have highlighted in our recent notes on the transition 
to mid-cycle, this phase is typically characterised by greater 
political headwinds . T he mid -term election  outcome 
reinforces the trend. The experience of 2015 -2017, 
highlighted in the second table, offers a useful historical 
parallel. Despite the knee-jerk reaction, investors should not 
overreact based on one day’s moves. While we had trimmed 
equities to a core allocation last week, we continue to expect 
equities to deliver mid-to-high single-digit returns per annum 
during the mid -cycle, even amid political headwinds, 
supported by steady underlying earnings growth in both 2027 
and 2028. 
So, what should investors do to navigate the next few months 
before the newly elected representatives are sworn in and 
take control of the House and Senate?  As we have been 
highlighting in recent notes, equity markets should still deliver 
positive returns, albeit at a more moderate pace. Hence, we 
recommend staying invested but diversification remains key  
to navigating the next phase of the cycle.  
FX: DXY near-term support at 100.7, 12 -month target at 
98.5. A Democratic sweep potentially removes the legislative 
runway for further tariff escalation and for fiscal expansion, 
the two structural pillars of US exceptionalism that have 
anchored dollar demand in 2026. Reduced fiscal impulse 
compresses the growth and yield differential that has kept the 
US dollar well-bid all year. Additionally, potential Democratic 
restrictions on AI and data centre investment could weigh on 
US equity outperformance, which is likely to act as a 
secondary dollar-negative channel.  
However, despite historical readings supporting a near -term 
DXY headwind from a Democratic sweep, the magnitude will

In the past few weeks, the US Treasury yield curve has 
broadly shifted downwards, after our opportunistic call to go 
long 10-year Treasuries at the end of September. We are of 
the view the ultra-long end downshift in yields is overdone 
(given the still challenging US fiscal trajectory) and would 
recommend investors who had earlier taken long duration 
exposures at the 20 to 30-year parts of the curve reduce their 
positions now. We continue to believe the 3 to 7-year part of 
the curve offers the most optimal balance of income and 
interest rate risk, particularly via investment corporate bonds.

of AI data centre buildouts could marginally improve market 
technicals, as hyperscaler and tech sector bond issuance 
may slow  (and reduce “crowding out” of other bond 
issuances). Conversely, from a credit perspective, we believe 
this dynamic may also make the data centre focused deals 
less viable, and  would be cautious on some of this sector’s 
lower quality names. Meanwhile, less AI capex dependent 
issuers continue to offer solid credit profiles amidst the still 
strong economy. High yield corporate spreads have widened 
in recent weeks but dispersion wi thin the complex is wide, 
with BBs and Bs credit spreads staying relatively resilient, 
while CCCs have widened to distressed levels. For income -
focused investors, we would recommend staying within the 
crossover range of between BBB-BB.

think the Mideast situation should de -escalate given 
Democratic oversight of the defense budget, and the likely re-
configuration of oil supply chains. On the margin, we are also 
closely monitoring regulatory developments in the US shale

2 of 8 October 19, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
The U.S. Capex Boom No One is Talking About—And Its Implications
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
We have long been of the belief that the U.S. is on the cusp of an inbound FDI capex boom
that can result in numerous positive spillover effects for the U.S. economy. The latest

figures from the U.S. Department of Commerce bear this out.
Exhibit 1 highlights the growing push among foreign multinationals to be inside the U.S.,
with FDI totaling nearly $240B in the first six months of this year. That is a staggering
103% jump from a year ago and places FDI inflows on track for a record year.
The latest inbound capex already adds to the massive scale of foreign investment in the
U.S. To wit, the accumulated stock of FDI in the U.S. stands at nearly $6T, based on the

latest figures from the Bureau of Economic Analysis (BEA). Manufacturing accounts for
roughly 43% of the total, and, at last count, some 10,000 foreign affiliates were operating
in the U.S.
These firms are largely invisible to the average U.S. investor, but the activities of U.S.
affiliates of foreign multinationals are substantial and competitive- and growth-enhancing.
To this point and based on the latest data from the BEA, foreign affiliates in the U.S.

employed nearly 8.6 million U.S. workers in 2024. That represents 6.1% of total private
sector employment, with manufacturing and retail the two most prominent sectors
of employment. Roughly 24% of all manufacturing jobs in the U.S. are from foreign
subsidiaries. In terms of pay, U.S. workers at these firms make $89,000 annually, 7% higher
than the national average, according to the Global Business Alliance.
Foreign affiliates also contributed some $1.5T to U.S. GDP; comprised some 15.7% of total

U.S. private sector investment; accounted for 12.4% of total U.S. research & development
(R&D) expenditures; paid roughly 20% of all U.S. federal taxes; and made up 22.5% of
U.S. exports and 26.2% of U.S. imports. 1 These are the direct effects of U.S. subsidiaries
of foreign companies operating in the U.S. The indirect effects are even greater in terms
of more income, jobs, growth and tax revenue—all bolstering the long-term competitive
advantage of the U.S. In a nutshell, foreign firms operating in the U.S. are an important

ingredient of U.S. economic growth.
There are multiple factors behind surging inflows: One, due to rising U.S. trade
protectionism, foreign multinationals increasingly want to be “inside” the U.S. economy,
not outside. Companies that once served the U.S. market exclusively through exports
increasingly face higher costs and more uncertainties and hence want to be “inside.”
Two, with a $32T-plus economy with extraordinary consumer purchasing power, foreign

firms prefer to be closer to their customers via investment than just trade (exports). There
is no more dynamic consumer in the world than the American shopper.
Three, America’s massive investment cycle in AI, data centers, semiconductors, power
generation, etc., has created numerous investment opportunities for many foreign firms
that don’t want to miss out on America’s surging capex cycle.
Four, and finally, abundant energy supplies, deep capital markets and a highly developed

technological ecosystem have also induced more FDI inflows.
As more U.S. affiliates of foreign multinationals set up shop in the U.S., think more job
creation across the country, more manufacturing output, more locally sourced purchases,
more U.S. exports, more spending on R&D, and more tax revenue for local communities.
And over the long run, think a more globally competitive U.S. economy.
1 Activities of U.S. Affiliates of Foreign Multinational Enterprises, 2024” and “Activities of U.S. Affiliates of Foreign

Multinational Enterprises, 2023”, BEA July 2026.
Investment Implications
The boom in foreign investment in
the U.S. should continue to support
employment, productivity, corporate
earnings, and broader U.S. economic
competitiveness, in our view. Hence
our U.S. bias.

### Page 3

3 of 8 October 19, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
And speaking of global competitiveness, Exhibit 2A is very illustrative of the magnet pull of
the U.S. economy to attract FDI versus the rest of the world. Of new greenfield investment
this decade, note the overriding preference for the U.S., accounting for nearly 16% of total
global greenfield investment this decade. America’s share is larger than the next three
countries combined—the United Kingdom, India, and Germany. And note that America’s

share of new investment is more than 5X that of China’s. Most of this new investment
has gone into manufacturing sectors like electrical equipment, computers, appliances and
batteries and has originated from Asia and Europe.
The bulk of foreign investment comes from Europe, followed by Japan and Canada, but as
Exhibit 2B highlights, there is a new dynamic playing out when it comes to America’s ability
to attract capital. There is a new source in town: a handful of developing nations (namely

Taiwan, South Korea, Mexico and Brazil). Mexican manufacturers, Taiwan technology
leaders, South Korea chip leaders, Brazil industrial firms, India pharmaceutical firms—these
companies and more are realizing that in today’s trade environment, the best way to serve
the American market is not necessarily through exports but through investment—investing
locally. FDI flows from developing nations reached $63B in the 12 months through June,
more than double the level a year prior ($24.5B), according to the BEA. Fueled by insatiable

AI demand, flows from South Korea alone totaled $23.4B in the first half of 2026. The
upshot is that an increasingly important source of capital for the U.S. is coming from an
unlikely source: the developing nations.
More fire for the capex boom. The coming boom in FDI inflows is supportive of our thesis
that the U.S. stands on the cusp of a major industrial upgrade. We are in the early innings
of a structural—not cyclical—capex cycle that should lead to the rebuilding of domestic

supply chains, higher value-added manufacturing, and more AI-driven productivity growth.
Foreign firms, in our opinion, will be part of this process.
That is important to recognize because remember the following: when a foreign company
builds a factory or an R&D facility or expands its existing manufacturing capacity right
here in the U.S., good things happen. Demand for U.S. goods and services goes up (as does
demand for construction, materials, logistics and professional services). New jobs are created,

boosting incomes of U.S. workers, while supporting more local spending and consumption.
And because many foreign firms often bring advanced manufacturing technologies when
investing in the U.S., the end results include a boost in productivity and innovation in various
sectors of the economy.
All the above, of course, are the secret ingredients to stronger U.S. economic growth, rising
corporate earnings and higher equity prices. With a little help from our friends, America’s

industrial renewal is gaining strength and momentum.

4 of 8 October 19, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MARKET VIEW
Europe’s Electoral Cycle and Market Risks
Ehiwario Efeyini, Director and Senior Investment Strategist
As midterm elections approach for the U.S. next month, voters across Europe will also
enter their own election season starting this November and continuing throughout
2027. Over the next year, 52% of the total EU population, representing 46% of EU GDP
across eight countries, will go to the polls, including voters in three of its four largest

economies (Exhibit 3). Having announced snap general elections earlier this month,
Spain will vote for members of its lower and upper house on November 29. A series of
parliamentary elections are then due for 2027 in Estonia (by March 7), Finland (on April
18), Greece (by July 25), Slovakia (by September 28), Poland (by November 11) and Italy
(by December 22). And perhaps most consequentially France will stage Presidential
elections on April 18, with a potential second round of voting set for May 2.

For the EU as a whole, the significance of these coming elections will be the potential
for a shift toward more populist, domestically oriented policy priorities from leading
opposition candidates (particularly in France, Spain and Poland) which could obstruct the
process of closer EU integration. In the nearer term, adoption of the new seven-year
(2028-2034) EU budget due by the end of 2027 could be impeded. And over the longer
term, renewed efforts toward an EU-wide capital market union to deepen and attract

more inward investment into European markets could also be further delayed.
At the country level, investors have seen bond yields rise globally in 2026 on the back of
higher inflation and concerns over fiscal sustainability. The debt question has arguably
been most pressing in parts of Europe, especially given the likelihood that upcoming
elections make for domestic increases in government largesse. And of the eight
countries going to the polls over the next year, France remains the biggest source of

concern. Interest rate spreads on French 10-year sovereign bonds have overtaken those
in both Greece and Italy this year (Exhibit 4), driven by investor concerns over public
debt. The 2027 government budget unveiled earlier this month projected a deficit of
5.0% of GDP (well in excess of the 3% specified in the EU Stability and Growth Pact),
but remained insufficiently expansive to nullify the risk that the current government
could be unseated later this year if it is forced through the fragmented parliament. This

comes on top of an existing debt load of close to 120% of GDP and a halving of official
2026 growth forecasts from 1.0% at the start of the year to 0.5% in September. In
short, all four variables in France’s debt sustainability equation currently stand as major
headwinds, and the two leading candidates in the 2027 election have shown limited
appetite for fiscal consolidation over the coming years.
Exhibit 3: Europe's Upcoming Elections Represent Around Half of EU GDP and Population.

Investment Implications
We remain cautious on European
markets overall, despite seeing
pockets of strength in selected
areas such as defense, data center
infrastructure and renewable energy.
On top of growing uncertainty
from the upcoming electoral cycle

and interest rate outlook, rising
competition and slowing demand
from China continue to challenge
key segments of the Consumer
Discretionary sector. And limited
direct AI concentration also leaves
Europe under-exposed to the key
growth theme that continues to buoy
equity markets in the U.S., Japan and
emerging Asia.
